Oh, well... I suppose I have been a little less active lately. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself, because I really do try to keep busy. 

But... well, I used to always look forward to my watercolor class, and the quilting circle, and my book club. They were such lovely groups of ladies. Lately, though... I'll get my bag all packed and ready, and then I'll just leave it sitting by the door. I just... I can't seem to make myself care enough to actually go out and do it.

Oh... well, let me think. I don't want to give you the wrong idea and make you think I've just been miserable my whole life, because I really haven't. I've had a very nice life, mostly.

Oh... well... hopeless is such a strong word, isn't it? I wouldn't want you to think I've given up or anything like that. I've always tried to look on the bright side, truly I have. 

But... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest—and I know you need me to be honest so you can help me—it has been a bit difficult lately. Most mornings I wake up with this... this heavy, low feeling. It's almost like... like the color has just gone out of the day before I even manage to get dressed. 

I know I shouldn't let myself feel that way, and I try to shake it off, I really do. I don't want to be a bother or sound like a complainer. It's just... a bit of a struggle to find the joy in things right now. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too dreary for you.

Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Well... I hate to complain, I really do. I know everyone gets a little blue sometimes, and I certainly don't want to sound ungrateful for what I have. But... yes, I suppose I have been feeling a bit down lately. \n\nIt's just... well, most mornings I wake up with this heavy, low feeling. It's almost like the color has just gone out of the day before I even manage to get dressed. I try my very best to shake it off and put on a brave face, I promise I do. I hope that doesn't make me sound terribly gloomy... is that the kind of thing you needed to know?\n\nDoctor: Have you been finding less pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose I have been a little less active lately. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself, because I really do try to keep busy. \n\nBut... well, I used to always look forward to my watercolor class, and the quilting circle, and my book club. They were such lovely groups of ladies. Lately, though... I'll get my bag all packed and ready, and then I'll just leave it sitting by the door. I just... I can't seem to make myself care enough to actually go out and do it. \n\nI know it sounds awful, and I'm sure they wonder where I am. I feel terrible about it, I really do. I just don't seem to have the spark for it right now. Is that... does that happen to other people sometimes? I hope you don't think I'm just being lazy.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well, let's see... I really hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is. I know time can sort of blur together when you're my age. But... I suppose this low, colorless stretch has been with me for... oh, maybe about a year and two months now? \n\nI kept thinking it would just pass, you know? Like a long winter that just overstayed its welcome. I really did try to just push through it and not make a fuss. I hope I haven't let it go on too long before saying something... am I being helpful telling you all this?\n\nDoctor: Before this stretch began, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt depressed like this for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Oh... well, let me think. I don't want to give you the wrong idea and make you think I've just been miserable my whole life, because I really haven't. I've had a very nice life, mostly. \n\nBut... no, I don't think I've ever had a time quite like this before. Not where it stayed so heavy for weeks on end. Usually, if I felt a bit down, a good night's sleep and a cup of tea would set me right again. I always tried to be the cheerful one, you know? \n\nSo... no, I don't believe I've had another spell like this one. I hope that's the right answer... I just want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need. Is that what you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Oh... well, no, I don't believe so. I mean, I try very hard not to be a worrywart. I've always thought fretting doesn't do anyone any good, you know? \n\nSo... no, I wouldn't say I've been feeling anxious or on edge. Just... mostly just that heavy, flat feeling we were talking about. I hope it's alright that I don't have that problem? I'd hate for you to think I'm holding anything back or not being thorough, but my nerves have actually been quite steady. Am I making sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost weight during this past year?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose I should mention my eating habits, since you're asking about weight. I really hope I don't sound foolish, because I know I should be taking better care of myself at my age. I've always prided myself on making proper meals, you know? \n\nBut... well, that's actually sort of what made me finally come in to see you. My eating has just gotten all turned around lately. I don't want you to think I'm completely neglecting myself! It's just... some days I'll realize I've completely forgotten to have lunch. And then other days, I'll just stand at the kitchen counter eating crackers and sweets, even though nothing really tastes right to me anymore. \n\nI know it's silly, and I'm trying to do better, I promise. Have I... have I let myself get too out of sorts with it? I just want to make sure I'm telling you what you need to know.\n\nDoctor: Have you unintentionally lost weight over the past year?\n\nPatient: Oh, I see... I suppose you're asking if my clothes are fitting differently because of what I just mentioned about my eating. I hope I didn't confuse things by bringing up my meals first. \n\nBut, well... yes, I suppose I have lost a little weight without really meaning to. It's just because things have gotten so mixed up, like I said. Forgetting meals and just snacking on those crackers when I do remember... it doesn't really add up to a proper diet, does it? I haven't been trying to lose weight, I promise. It's just that with nothing tasting quite right, the weight sort of slipped off on its own. \n\nI hope that's not too concerning? I really am going to try to be better about making proper dinners again. Did I explain that alright for you?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless lately?\n\nPatient: Oh... well... hopeless is such a strong word, isn't it? I wouldn't want you to think I've given up or anything like that. I've always tried to look on the bright side, truly I have. \n\nBut... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest—and I know you need me to be honest so you can help me—it has been a bit difficult lately. Most mornings I wake up with this... this heavy, low feeling. It's almost like... like the color has just gone out of the day before I even manage to get dressed. \n\nI know I shouldn't let myself feel that way, and I try to shake it off, I really do. I don't want to be a bother or sound like a complainer. It's just... a bit of a struggle to find the joy in things right now. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too dreary for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... tired? I suppose everyone my age gets a little tired now and then, don't they? I wouldn't want you to think I'm just sitting around the house all day doing nothing. I always try to keep busy, keep the house tidy, you know. \n\nI haven't really noticed feeling unusually exhausted or anything like that. I mean, maybe I slow down a bit in the afternoons, but that's perfectly normal, isn't it? I don't think I'm lacking energy any more than anyone else would be. I really do try to push through and keep active. I hope that's the right answer? I just wouldn't want to make a fuss over normal everyday tiredness.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, my sleep? Well... I think I sleep just fine, really. I mean, as well as anyone my age does, I suppose. 

I don't want you to think I'm tossing and turning all night or keeping anyone awake. I always make sure to go to bed at a sensible hour, and I usually just close my eyes and... well, I rest. Sometimes I might wake up for a moment to use the restroom, but that's just a normal part of getting older, isn't it? 

I really don't think I have any trouble sleeping. I hope that's alright? I try to make sure I'm well-rested so I can be productive the next day. Did I answer that the way you needed?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, concentrating? Well... I suppose I manage alright for the most part. I don't want you to think I'm losing my mind or getting dotty, you know. I always try to stay sharp and keep on top of my chores. 

But... if I'm being perfectly honest—and I know you want me to be—it has been a little tricky sometimes. Just silly little things, really. Like... I'll sit there staring at something simple, trying to decide whether to call the pharmacy or open the mail first. And my mind just gets so... so foggy, I suppose? I end up just sitting there and doing neither of them. 

I know I shouldn't let myself get so muddled, and I feel terrible about it. I don't want to sound like I can't take care of myself. It's just a little harder to keep my thoughts straight right now. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not giving you the wrong impression.
